We can exploit homogeneity and use multiplicative gradient/mirror: Let S=1. Define L(a) = log(∑_k exp(β b_k)) as surrogate for max; optimizing L. Gradient ∂L/∂a_i = (1/Z) ∑_k w_k ∂b_k/∂a_i where w_k=exp(βb_k)/Z. ∂b_k/∂a_i = 2 a_{k-i} (valid). So gradient = 2 * (w * a) linear convolution? Let's design a custom optimizer:

Function fast_convolve(a): use rfft size L >= len(a)*2-1. Since a length n, full conv length 2n-1. Use np.fft.rfft(a, n=N), square, irfft. Because self-conv: C=(rfft(a,N)**2 real?) Actually linear convolution: A=fft(a,2n-1); B=A*A; ifft gives circular convolution length 2n-1; since length exactly full, no overlap if N>=2n-1. For real a, conv = np.fft.irfft(A*A, N)[:2n-1]. Or use rfft; A= rfft(a,N); C= A*A; irfft C returns length N, real. For self-conv of real, A*A (not A*conj) because same; irfft expects conjugate symmetric coefficients of a real sequence? The DFT of a real signal is conjugate symmetric; pointwise product A*A is also conjugate symmetric; inverse via irfft okay. However A from rfft is half spectrum; A*A fine. Use N=2*n? next power of two. For n=8192, N=16384, conv length 16383, okay.

But p normalized sum1. b=conv. max_b. Score=2*n*max_b (since sum1). Note evaluate_sequence normalizes by sum^2; if p sum1, same.

Gradient:

Let's design high-quality code.

Key functions:
```
def _next_pow2(m):
    return 1 << (int(m)-1).bit_length()

def fast_self_convolve(a):
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.array([])
    if n <= 512:
       return np.convolve(a,a)
    N=_next_pow2(2*n-1)
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    C=np.fft.irfft(A*A,N)
    return C[:2*n-1]
```
For gradient H, need convolve reversed p with W length 2n-1. Use direct if n small else FFT.
```
def hankel_product(p,w):
   n=len(p); np_full=p[::-1]
   L=n+len(w)-1
   if n*len(w) < 200000 maybe direct? Direct: for i in range(n): sum p[j]*w[i+j]. Could vectorize mat? Not.
   FFT N2=next_pow2(L)
   A=rfft(np_full,N2); B=rfft(w,N2); C=irfft(A*B,N2); return C[n-1:n-1+n]
```
Check `w` length 2n-1.

```
def binomial_ratios(n):
    r=np.empty(n)
    val=1.0; r[0]=1.0
    for k in range(1,n): val *= (2*k-1)/(2*k); r[k]=val
    return r
```

Implementation of `fast_score`:
```
def fast_score(seq):
    a=np.asarray(seq,dtype=float)
    if a.size==0 or not finite: return np.inf
    s=a.sum()
    if s <=0: return np.inf
    if s !=1: a=a/s
    c=fast_self_convolve(a)
    if c.size==0: return np.inf
    return float(2*a.size*max(c))
```
Note if a not normalized, better normalize to sum1. evaluate_sequence score invariant to scaling; if values huge clipped? But in our search values small. We normalize.

Potential exact evaluator for our normalized p (sum1). It clips max(0) and min(1000), no issue. sum_a=1 ≥0.01. score=2n max exact. Good.

Now, if `evaluate_sequence` uses exact np.convolve, for n=10000 with normalized p values ~0.01? Sum1, some values ~0.031? Products ~1e-3, conv sums ~1/n=1e-4; double precision fine.

Let's detail optimize function:
```
def optimize_sequence(p, deadline, verbose=True):
    p = np.asarray(p,float).copy()
    p = np.clip(p,0,None)
    s=p.sum(); if s<=0: return p,np.inf
    p /= s
    n=p.size
    theta = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))
    theta -= theta.mean()
    m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n)
    best_p=p.copy(); best_s=fast_score(p); t=1
    lr=0.08
    no_improve=0
    start=time.monotonic()
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        # compute conv and weights
        c=fast_self_convolve(p)
        if c.size:
            # Clean tiny negatives
            # c = np.where(c>0,c,0)
            max_b=np.max(c)
            if not finite: break
            score=2*n*max_b
            if score < best_s - 1e-12:
                best_s=score; best_p=p.copy(); no_improve=0
            else:
                no_improve += 1
            if max_b > 0:
                # anneal factor based on elapsed
                elapsed=time.monotonic()-start
                budget= max(1.0, deadline-start)
                prog=min(1.0, elapsed/budget)
                spread = 3.0 + (400.0)*prog*prog
                tau = max_b / spread
                d = max_b - c
                # negative d ignored; clip exponent
                x = d / tau
                x = np.where(x < 40.0, x, 40.0)
                w = np.exp(-x)
                wsum=w.sum()
                if wsum <=0: w=np.zeros_like(c); w[np.argmax(c)]=1
                else: w/=wsum
                H = hankel_product(p,w)
                norm = np.dot(p,H)
                grad = p*(H - norm)
                # gradient may be very small; use
                t_cur = t
                beta1=0.9; beta2=0.999
                m = beta1*m + (1-beta1)*grad
                v = beta2*v + (1-beta2)*grad*grad
                mhat = m/(1-beta1**t_cur)
                vhat = v/(1-beta2**t_cur)
                step = lr * mhat/(np.sqrt(vhat)+1e-8)
                # clip step to avoid huge
                step=np.clip(step, -1.0, 1.0)
                theta -= step
                # occasionally re-center
                if t_cur % 100 ==0:
                    theta -= theta.mean()
                u=theta-np.max(theta)
                p=np.exp(np.clip(u,-700,700))
                ps=p.sum()
                if not finite or ps<=0:
                    p=best_p.copy(); theta=np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-300)); theta-=theta.mean(); m=m*0; v=v*0
                else:
                    p/=ps
        t +=1
        # restart if stuck and enough time
        if no_improve > 1000 and (time.monotonic()-start) > 60 and (deadline - time.monotonic()) > 120:
             # perturb best
             p = best_p.copy()
             rng_local = np.random.default_rng(12345+t) # need top-level? no closure; can create inside? It's a function not closure.
             # random block scale
             width=max(1,n//20)
             idx=int(rng_local.integers(0,n-width))
             p[idx:idx+width] *= float(np.exp(rng_local.normal(0,0.2,width)))
             p=np.clip(p,0,None); ps=p.sum(); if ps>0: p/=ps
             theta=np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-300)); theta-=theta.mean()
             m=np.zeros_like(p); v=np.zeros_like(p); no_improve=0; t=1
    return best_p,best_s
```

```
def hankel_product(p,w):
    n=p.size
    N = next_power_of_two(n + w.size -1)
    rp = p[::-1]
    F = np.fft.rfft(rp, N)
    G = np.fft.rfft(w, N)
    C = np.fft.irfft(F*G, N)
    return C[n-1:n-1+n]
```

Let's write final code now mentally.

```
import numpy as np
import time

def _next_pow2(m):
    if m <=1: return 1
    return 1 << (int(m)-1).bit_length()

def _fast_self_convolve(a):
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0: return np.empty(0, dtype=float)
    if n <= 256:
        return np.convolve(a, a)
    N = _next_pow2(2*n-1)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    C = np.fft.irfft(A*A, N)
    return C[:2*n-1]

def _fast_score(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.ndim !=1 or a.size==0: return np.inf
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)): return np.inf
    a = np.where(a>0,a,0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300: return np.inf
    a = a/s
    conv = _fast_self_convolve(a)
    if conv.size==0: return np.inf
    mb = float(np.max(conv))
    if not np.isfinite(mb): return np.inf
    return float(2.0*a.size*mb)

def _hankel_product(p,w):
    n=p.size
    L = n + w.size -1
    if L <=0: return np.empty(0)
    N=_next_pow2(L)
    rp = p[::-1]
    A=np.fft.rfft(rp,N)
    B=np.fft.rfft(w,N)
    C=np.fft.irfft(A*B,N)
    return C[n-1:n-1+n]
```

Check `w.size - L`? If L=3n-1, N next pow.

`central`:
```
def _central_binomial_normalized(n):
    r=np.empty(n, dtype=float)
    val=1.0; r[0]=1.0
    for k in range(1,n):
       val *= (2*k-1)/(2*k)
       r[k]=val
    s=r.sum()
    if s<=0: return np.ones(n)/n
    return r/s
```
For n=0? not.

`shifted_tail_normalized`:
```
def _tail_initial(n, shift, max_shift_extra=50):
   # precompute central d up to n+shift maybe; reuse? To avoid repeated, precompute once in propose.
```
Simpler in propose: compute `d = _central_ratios(n + max_shift)`; for shift `r=d[shift:shift+n]`, normalize.

Function `_central_ratios(n)` returns unnormalized r. Use for shifts. We need central p = ratios[n]? d[0:n]. Good.

`_optimize`:
```
def _optimize_sequence(p, deadline, rng):
   p = np.asarray(...).copy()
   p=np.where(p>0,p,0.0)
   s=p.sum(); if s<=0: return p, np.inf
   p/=s
   n=p.size
   best_p=p.copy(); best_s=_fast_score(p)
   if n<2: return best_p,best_s
   theta=np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-300)); theta-=theta.mean()
   m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); bias1=1.0; bias2=1.0
   lr=0.10; it=0; no_imp=0; start=time.monotonic(); last_print=start
   while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       c=_fast_self_convolve(p)
       if c.size==0: break
       max_b=float(np.max(c))
       if not np.isfinite(max_b): break
       score=float(2*n*max_b)
       if score < best_s - 1e-13:
          best_s=score; best_p=p.copy(); no_imp=0
       else:
          no_imp +=1
       if max_b <= 0: break
       elapsed=time.monotonic()-start
       total=max(1.0, deadline-start)
       prog=min(1.0, elapsed/total)
       spread = 4.0 + 500.0*prog*prog
       tau = max_b / spread
       d = max_b - c
       # d may negative (if c>max? no), if negative set 0?
       with np.errstate(over='ignore', under='ignore'):
           x = d / tau
       x = np.where(x < 40.0, x, 40.0)
       # if x negative (c>max due rounding), exp positive; maybe set x=0 if x<0
       x = np.where(x > 0.0, x, 0.0)
       w = np.exp(-x)
       ws = w.sum()
       if not np.isfinite(ws) or ws <= 0:
           w = np.zeros_like(c); w[int(np.argmax(c))]=1.0
       else:
           w /= ws
       H = _hankel_product(p,w)
       # H may have tiny nan? clean
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(H)):
           H = np.zeros(n)
       norm = float(np.dot(p,H))
       grad = p*(H - norm)
       grad = np.where(np.isfinite(grad), grad, 0.0)
       it +=1
       bias1 *=0.9
       bias2 *=0.999
       m = 0.9*m + 0.1*grad
       v = 0.999*v + 0.001*grad*grad
       if 1.0-bias1 > 0:
           mhat = m/(1.0-bias1)
       else: mhat=m
       if 1.0-bias2 >0:
           vhat = v/(1.0-bias2)
       else: vhat=v
       step = lr * mhat / (np.sqrt(vhat) + 1e-8)
       step = np.where(np.isfinite(step), step, 0.0)
       np.clip(step, -0.5, 0.5, out=step)
       theta -= step
       if it % 200 ==0:
           theta -= theta.mean()
       tmp = theta - np.max(theta)
       tmp = np.where(np.isfinite(tmp), tmp, -700.0)
       tmp = np.clip(tmp, -700.0, 700.0)
       newp = np.exp(tmp)
       news = newp.sum()
       if not np.isfinite(newsp) or news <=0:
           p = best_p.copy()
           theta = np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-300)); theta -= theta.mean()
           m = np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); bias1=1; bias2=1; no_imp=0
       else:
           p = newp / news
       if time.monotonic() - last_print > 20:
           print(f"    opt it={it} elapsed={time.monotonic()-start:.1f}s best_fft={best_s:.9f}")
           last_print = time.monotonic()
       # restart if stuck
       if no_imp > 1200 and (time.monotonic()-start) > 120 and (deadline-time.monotonic()) > 180:
           # perturb best with a block
           p = best_p.copy()
           width = max(1, n//25)
           idx = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n-width+1)))
           stop = min(n, idx+width)
           p[idx:stop] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.25, stop-idx)))
           p = np.where(p>0,p,0.0)
           ps=p.sum()
           if ps>0: p/=ps
           else: p=best_p.copy()
           theta=np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-300)); theta-=theta.mean()
           m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); bias1=1.0; bias2=1.0; no_imp=0
           print(f"    restart at {time.monotonic()-start:.1f}s best={best_s:.9f}")
   return best_p,best_s
```

Implementation of final scaling:
```
def _scale_for_eval(a, max_val=500.0, max_scale=1e6):
   a=np.asarray(a,float)
   a=np.where(a>0,a,0.0)
   m=float(np.max(a)) if a.size else 0.0
   if not np.isfinite(m) or m <=0: return a
   scale=max_val/m
   if not np.isfinite(scale) or scale <=0: return a
   scale=min(scale, max_scale)
   b=a*scale
   b=np.clip(b,0.0,1000.0)
   return b
```
If m=0, return zeros? evaluator inf; but not.

```
"""docstring"""
import numpy as np
import time

... helper functions ...

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    start=time.monotonic()
    try: budget=float(budget_s)
    except: budget=2400.0
    budget=max(60.0,budget)
    final_reserve=120.0
    deadline=start+budget-final_reserve
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    n = int(kwargs.get('length', 10000))
    if height_sequence exists maybe if better analyze? But we can set n maybe.
    # Decide length: choose default or length from kwargs. If height_sequence has length and score promising? not.
    n = int(max(1024, min(n, 20000)))
    # Precompute central ratios up to n+max_shift
    max_shift = min(64, n//4)
    d = _central_ratios(n+max_shift)
    best_seq=None; best_score=np.inf
    shifts...
    # Optionally evaluate height_sequence_1
    hs = globals().get('height_sequence_1', None)
    if hs is not None:
       try:
          arr=np.asarray(hs, dtype=float)
          arr=np.where(np.isfinite(arr) & (arr>0), arr, 0.0)
          if arr.size>=256 and arr.sum()>0.01:
             sc=_fast_score(arr)
             print('height_sequence_1 fft score', sc)
             if sc < best_score - 1e-6:
                 best_score=sc; best_seq=arr/arr.sum()
                 n=arr.size
                 # if length changed, maybe regenerate? We can optimize using this seq as initial; no central needed.
       except Exception as e: print...
    if best_seq is None: use central best from shifts.
    # If n changed due to height, still okay.
    # Optionally random perturb of best
    for _ in range(5): cand = best_seq.copy(); width=...; multiply; normalize; sc; update.
    initial_seq = best_seq.copy()
    # optimize
    optimized, opt_score = _optimize_sequence(best_seq, deadline, rng)
    # choose best normalized by FFT
    if opt_score < best_score: best_seq=optimized; best_score=opt_score
    else: best_seq=initial_seq
    # exact validation
    final_candidates=[best_seq, initial_seq]
    # maybe central seq for n
    if n <= 12000 and time.monotonic() < start+budget-60:
       exact_best_seq=None; exact_best=np.inf
       # To avoid duplicate exact same object? not critical.
       for seq in final_candidates:
          seq_eval=_scale_for_eval(seq)
          try: sc=evaluate_sequence(seq_eval.tolist())
          except Exception: sc=np.inf
          if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < exact_best:
              exact_best=sc; exact_best_seq=seq_eval
              print
       if exact_best_seq is not None:
          return [float(x) for x in exact_best_seq]
    # If no exact, scale best
    seq_eval=_scale_for_eval(best_seq)
    print('return without exact score due to limits')
    return [float(x) for x in seq_eval]
```

```python
"""
Low-merit nonnegative self-convolution sequences.

The main construction is the central-binomial sequence
    a_k = C(2k,k) / 4^k,
which has the infinite identity P(z)^2 = 1/(1-z).  Truncating to length n
gives a nonnegative sequence whose self-convolution peak is attained in the
first n lags and is especially low for this problem.  We build several
central-binomial/tail variants, then refine the best one with an FFT-based
log-sum-exp Adam in log-space.  Finally we rescale for numerical robustness
and validate with the exact evaluator when time permits.
"""

import numpy as np
import time


def _next_pow2(m):
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (int(m) - 1).bit_length()


def _fast_self_convolve(a):
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if n <= 256:
        return np.convolve(a, a)
    N = _next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    C = np.fft.irfft(A * A, N)
    return C[:2 * n - 1]


def _fast_score(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.ndim != 1 or a.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        return float("inf")
    a = np.where(a > 0.0, a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return float("inf")
    a = a / s
    c = _fast_self_convolve(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    mb = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(mb):
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * a.size * mb)


def _hankel_product(p, w):
    """
    H_i = sum_j p_j w_{i+j}, for i=0..n-1.
    Computed by convolving reversed p with w and slicing.
    """
    n = int(p.size)
    L = n + int(w.size) - 1
    if L <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    N = _next_pow2(L)
    rp = p[::-1]
    A = np.fft.rfft(rp, N)
    B = np.fft.rfft(w, N)
    C = np.fft.irfft(A * B, N)
    return C[n - 1:n - 1 + n]


def _central_ratios(m):
    """Unnormalized central-binomial coefficients C(2k,k)/4^k."""
    out = np.empty(m, dtype=np.float64)
    val = 1.0
    for k in range(m):
        if k > 0:
            val *= (2.0 * k - 1.0) / (2.0 * k)
        out[k] = val
    return out


def _normalized(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    a = np.where(a > 0.0, a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return None
    return a / s


def _scale_for_eval(a, max_val=500.0, max_scale=1e6):
    """
    Scale a normalized sequence to a comfortable amplitude for exact float
    evaluation.  The objective is scaling invariant, but larger moderate
    values reduce relative rounding and avoid tiny numbers.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    a = np.where(a > 0.0, a, 0.0)
    if a.size == 0:
        return a
    m = float(np.max(a))
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m <= 0.0:
        return a
    scale = max_val / m
    if not np.isfinite(scale) or scale <= 0.0:
        return a
    scale = min(scale, max_scale)
    b = a * scale
    b = np.clip(b, 0.0, 1000.0)
    return b


def _optimize_sequence(p, deadline, rng):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    p = np.where(p > 0.0, p, 0.0)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return p, float("inf")
    p = p / s
    n = int(p.size)
    best_p = p.copy()
    best_s = _fast_score(p)
    if n < 2:
        return best_p, best_s

    theta = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))
    theta -= float(np.mean(theta))
    m = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    bias1 = 1.0
    bias2 = 1.0
    lr = 0.10
    it = 0
    no_improve = 0
    start = time.monotonic()
    last_print = start

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        c = _fast_self_convolve(p)
        if c.size == 0:
            break
        max_b = float(np.max(c))
        if not np.isfinite(max_b) or max_b <= 0.0:
            break
        score = float(2.0 * n * max_b)
        if score < best_s - 1e-13:
            best_s = score
            best_p = p.copy()
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            no_improve += 1

        # Soft weights emphasizing current peaks.  Spread grows during run.
        elapsed = time.monotonic() - start
        total = max(1.0, deadline - start)
        prog = min(1.0, elapsed / total)
        spread = 4.0 + 500.0 * prog * prog
        tau = max_b / spread
        d = max_b - c
        with np.errstate(over="ignore", under="ignore", invalid="ignore"):
            x = d / tau
        x = np.where(np.isfinite(x), x, 40.0)
        x = np.where(x < 0.0, 0.0, x)
        x = np.where(x > 40.0, 40.0, x)
        w = np.exp(-x)
        ws = float(w.sum())
        if not np.isfinite(ws) or ws <= 0.0:
            w = np.zeros_like(c)
            w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        else:
            w /= ws

        H = _hankel_product(p, w)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(H)):
            H = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        norm = float(np.dot(p, H))
        grad = p * (H - norm)
        grad = np.where(np.isfinite(grad), grad, 0.0)

        it += 1
        bias1 *= 0.9
        bias2 *= 0.999
        m = 0.9 * m + 0.1 * grad
        v = 0.999 * v + 0.001 * (grad * grad)
        one_b1 = 1.0 - bias1
        one_b2 = 1.0 - bias2
        if one_b1 > 0.0:
            mhat = m / one_b1
        else:
            mhat = m
        if one_b2 > 0.0:
            vhat = v / one_b2
        else:
            vhat = v

        step = lr * mhat / (np.sqrt(vhat) + 1e-8)
        step = np.where(np.isfinite(step), step, 0.0)
        step = np.clip(step, -0.5, 0.5)
        theta -= step

        if it % 200 == 0:
            theta -= float(np.mean(theta))

        u = theta - float(np.max(theta))
        u = np.where(np.isfinite(u), u, -70.0)
        u = np.clip(u, -70.0, 0.0)
        newp = np.exp(u)
        news = float(newp.sum())
        if not np.isfinite(news) or news <= 0.0:
            p = best_p.copy()
            theta = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))
            theta -= float(np.mean(theta))
            m = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
            v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
            bias1 = 1.0
            bias2 = 1.0
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            p = newp / news

        if time.monotonic() - last_print > 20.0:
            print(f"    opt it={it} elapsed={time.monotonic()-start:.1f}s best_fft={best_s:.9f}")
            last_print = time.monotonic()

        if no_improve > 1200 and (time.monotonic() - start) > 120 and (deadline - time.monotonic()) > 180:
            p = best_p.copy()
            width = max(1, n // 25)
            idx = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width + 1)))
            stop = min(n, idx + width)
            p[idx:stop] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.25, stop - idx)))
            p = np.where(p > 0.0, p, 0.0)
            ps = float(p.sum())
            if ps > 0.0:
                p = p / ps
            else:
                p = best_p.copy()
            theta = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))
            theta -= float(np.mean(theta))
            m = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
            v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
            bias1 = 1.0
            bias2 = 1.0
            no_improve = 0
            print(f"    restart at elapsed={time.monotonic()-start:.1f}s best_fft={best_s:.9f}")

    return best_p, best_s


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    start = time.monotonic()
    try:
        raw_budget = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        raw_budget = 2400.0
    if not np.isfinite(raw_budget) or raw_budget <= 0.0:
        raw_budget = 2400.0
    raw_budget = max(60.0, raw_budget)

    if raw_budget > 1000.0:
        reserve = 120.0
    elif raw_budget > 300.0:
        reserve = 60.0
    else:
        reserve = max(3.0, 0.1 * raw_budget)
    deadline = start + max(1.0, raw_budget - reserve)

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    n = int(kwargs.get("length", 10000))
    n = int(max(256, min(n, 20000)))

    print(f"propose_candidate: budget={raw_budget:.1f}s target_n={n}")

    # Primary family: central-binomial tails.
    max_shift = min(64, max(0, n // 4))
    d = _central_ratios(n + max_shift)

    best_seq = None
    best_score = float("inf")

    shifts = [0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 18, 24, 32, 48]
    for sh in shifts:
        if sh >= n:
            continue
        seq = _normalized(d[sh:sh + n])
        if seq is None:
            continue
        sc = _fast_score(seq)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_seq = seq.copy()
            print(f"  shift={sh} n={n} fft_score={sc:.9f}")

    # If the externally provided sequence is surprisingly good, use it.
    hs = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    if hs is not None:
        try:
            arr = np.asarray(hs, dtype=np.float64)
            arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr) & (arr > 0.0), arr, 0.0)
            if arr.size >= 256 and arr.size <= 20000 and arr.sum() > 0.01:
                seq = _normalized(arr)
                if seq is not None:
                    sc = _fast_score(seq)
                    if np.isfinite(sc):
                        print(f"  provided height_sequence_1 fft_score={sc:.9f}")
                        if sc < best_score - 1e-6:
                            best_score = sc
                            best_seq = seq.copy()
                            n = int(seq.size)
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"  could not use height_sequence_1: {e}")

    if best_seq is None:
        best_seq = _normalized(d[0:n])
        if best_seq is None:
            best_seq = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / n
        best_score = _fast_score(best_seq)

    n = int(best_seq.size)

    # Small stochastic initial diversions around the best analytic family.
    initial_seq = best_seq.copy()
    for _ in range(5):
        cand = best_seq.copy()
        width = max(1, n // 100)
        idx = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width + 1)))
        stop = min(n, idx + width)
        cand[idx:stop] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.15, stop - idx)))
        cand = np.where(cand > 0.0, cand, 0.0)
        s = float(cand.sum())
        if s > 0.0:
            cand = cand / s
            sc = _fast_score(cand)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                best_score = sc
                best_seq = cand.copy()
                print(f"  perturbed initial fft_score={sc:.9f}")

    # Refine by nonlinear optimization.
    optimized_seq, optimized_score = _optimize_sequence(best_seq, deadline, rng)
    if np.isfinite(optimized_score) and optimized_score < best_score:
        best_score = optimized_score
        best_seq = optimized_seq.copy()
    else:
        best_seq = initial_seq.copy()

    print(f"  best_fft_before_exact={best_score:.9f}")

    # Exact validation, if length is reasonable and time remains.
    exact_best_seq = None
    exact_best_score = float("inf")
    EXACT_N_LIMIT = 12000

    if n <= EXACT_N_LIMIT and (time.monotonic() < deadline + reserve * 0.75):
        final_candidates = []
        final_candidates.append(best_seq)
        final_candidates.append(initial_seq)
        # Also include the pure central-binomial sequence of this length.
        cb = _normalized(_central_ratios(n)[0:n])
        if cb is not None:
            final_candidates.append(cb)

        seen = []
        for seq in final_candidates:
            key_len = int(seq.size)
            key_sum = float(seq.sum())
            duplicated = False
            for k in seen:
                if k[0] == key_len and abs(k[1] - key_sum) <= 1e-12:
                    duplicated = True
                    break
            if duplicated:
                continue
            seen.append((key_len, key_sum))

            eval_seq = _scale_for_eval(seq)
            try:
                sc = evaluate_sequence(eval_seq.tolist())
                sc = float(sc)
            except Exception as e:
                print(f"  exact evaluation failed: {e}")
                sc = float("inf")
            print(f"  exact evaluate n={key_len} score={sc:.9f}")
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < exact_best_score:
                exact_best_score = sc
                exact_best_seq = eval_seq.copy()

    if exact_best_seq is not None:
        print(f"  return exact-validated score={exact_best_score:.9f}")
        return [float(x) for x in exact_best_seq]

    # Fallback: scaled normalized best sequence.
    out = _scale_for_eval(best_seq)
    print(f"  return best_fft={best_score:.9f} without exact validation")
    return [float(x) for x in out]
```
